Adam at lr .001 was the strongest choice here, ahead of RMSprop at .0003. The gold order agrees with these existing claims; no new general rule is established.
hypothesis: -

### q_35b0c6 (ranking_v3, ranking, multivariate_regression) score=0.00 noKB_score=0.25 gold=E<B<D<C<A pred=B<A<E<C<D cited=K1006,K1008,K1015,K1080 retrieved=none
explanation: <answer>B<A<E<C<D</answer> <cited>K1080,K1008,K1006,K1015</cited>
comment: The decisive pattern was activation and residual signal propagation: residual ReLU (E) beat shallow ReLU (B), while residual SiLU (D) beat both nonresidual GELU models; among GELU choices, deeper C beat wider/shallow A. I over-applied K1080's multivariate depth caution and shallow preference, and treated residuals as weak per K1015. K1008's rectifier-over-smooth MSE tendency was directionally useful, but I failed to combine it with residual/depth benefits. The resulting ranking wrongly prioritized B and A.
hypothesis: -

### q_332c21 (dataflip500, select, univariate_regression) score=1.00 noKB_score=1.00 gold=B pred=B cited=K1008,K1039 retrieved=none
explanation: B is favored because LeakyReLU learns nonlinear regression features more efficiently under this low-rate momentum SGD recipe. A's unnormalized SiLU network is likely to fit mainly broad trends; residual connections do not remove that activation disadvantage. C adds one LayerNorm, but its deeper SiLU stack and unnormalized tail slow signal propagation. In this SGD regime, B's rectifier advantage outweighs its one-LayerNorm deficit to C.
comment: K1039 supplied the decisive scoped preference: with shared low-rate SGD on MSE regression, rectifiers can outweigh a one-block LayerNorm deficit against smooth activations. B's LeakyReLU therefore favored it over C despite C's single LN, while K1008 supported B over zero-LN SiLU A at equal LN count. C's depth and A's residual connections did not justify overriding that activation evidence. The gold confirms this application, but does not isolate activation from the bundled depth, width, and residual differences. No new general hypothesis is warranted.
hypothesis: -

### q_c9cbae (ranking_v2, ranking, xor_classification) score=0.75 noKB_score=0.75 gold=B<C<E<A<D pred=C<B<E<A<D cited=K1002,K1012,K1018,K1019 retrieved=none
explanation: <cited>K1002,K1012,K1018,K1019</cited>
<answer>C<B<E<A<D</answer>
comment: B’s residual, unnormalized LeakyReLU–Adagrad recipe beat C’s normalized SiLU–AdamW recipe despite nominal progress proxies of 0.192 versus 1.024. I overextended K1002’s same-architecture optimizer preference and K1018’s shared-optimizer smoothness tendency across differing recipes. K1001/K1012 correctly warn that greater displacement need not improve held-out CE after substantial training. Adagrad’s decaying updates might improve late-stage generalization, but the gold ordering does not establish that mechanism. Backtesting a broader long-budget Adagrad preference yielded only 3 hits and 6 misses across four history questions. In the lab, Adagrad .003 beat Adam-family .001 in only 11/48 comparisons across 25 sets. No tested general rule explains B’s advantage here.
hypothesis: -

### q_451036 (ranking_v3, ranking, univariate_regression) score=0.50 noKB_score=0.25 gold=C<B<D<A<E pred=C<D<A<B<E cited=K1006,K1008,K1012 retrieved=none
explanation: <cited>K1006,K1008,K1012</cited>
<answer>C<D<A<B<E</answer>
comment: C’s rectifier architecture ranked first, but B’s three consecutive early-normalized GELU blocks beat both D’s deeper four-LN SiLU network and A’s later-normalized GELU layout. I incorrectly promoted LN count and later placement into decisive tie-breakers. K1006 helped identify E’s disadvantage but explicitly disallows deterministic LN scoring; K1008 supported C; K1022 excludes univariate regression. Backtesting an “earlier LN wins” rule found two eligible history comparisons, zero hits, and no matched lab placement pairs. Conversely, more-LN smooth residual candidates won 6/6 same-type/rate lab comparisons across six sets, but none matched this depth/layout/budget bundle. Thus B’s advantage is recipe-specific evidence, not an established mechanism; normalization-dependent feature conditioning remains an untested explanation.
hypothesis: -
